2899</div></td></tr><tr class="mergedbottomrow"><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/ISO_3166" title="ISO 3166">ISO 3166 code</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/ISO_3166-2:NF" title="ISO 3166-2:NF">NF</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Country_code_top-level_domain" title="Country code top-level domain">Internet TLD</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/.nf" title=".nf">.nf</a></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><b>Norfolk Island</b> (<span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;n&#39; in &#39;nigh&#39;">n</span><span title="/ɔːr/: &#39;ar&#39; in &#39;war&#39;">ɔːr</span><span title="&#39;f&#39; in &#39;find&#39;">f</span><span title="/ə/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;about&#39;">ə</span><span title="&#39;k&#39; in &#39;kind&#39;">k</span></span>/</a></span></span>, <span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><small>locally </small><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;n&#39; in &#39;nigh&#39;">n</span><span title="/ɔːr/: &#39;ar&#39; in &#39;war&#39;">ɔːr</span><span title="&#39;f&#39; in &#39;find&#39;">f</span><span title="/oʊ/: &#39;o&#39; in &#39;code&#39;">oʊ</span><span title="&#39;k&#39; in &#39;kind&#39;">k</span></span>/</a></span></span>;<sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Norfuk_language" title="Norfuk language">Norfuk</a>: <i>Norf'k Ailen</i><sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup>) is an  <a href="/wiki/States_and_territories_of_Australia" title="States and territories of Australia">external territory</a> of <a href="/wiki/Australia" title="Australia">Australia</a> located in the Pacific Ocean between <a href="/wiki/New_Zealand" title="New Zealand">New Zealand</a> and <a href="/wiki/New_Caledonia" title="New Caledonia">New Caledonia</a>, 1,412 kilometres (877&#160;mi) directly east of Australia's <a href="/wiki/Evans_Head,_New_South_Wales" title="Evans Head, New South Wales">Evans Head</a> and about 900 kilometres (560&#160;mi) from <a href="/wiki/Lord_Howe_Island" title="Lord Howe Island">Lord Howe Island</a>. Together with the neighbouring <a href="/wiki/Phillip_Island_(Norfolk_Island)" title="Phillip Island (Norfolk Island)">Phillip Island</a> and <a href="/wiki/Nepean_Island_(Norfolk_Island)" title="Nepean Island (Norfolk Island)">Nepean Island</a>, the three islands collectively form the <b>Territory of Norfolk Island</b>.<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> At the <a href="/wiki/2021_Australian_census" title="2021 Australian census">2021 census</a>, it had <span class="nowrap"><span data-sort-value="7003218800000000000♠"></span>2188</span> inhabitants living on a total area of about 35&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (14&#160;sq&#160;mi).<sup id="cite_ref-ABS2021_6-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ABS2021-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> Its capital is <a href="/wiki/Kingston,_Norfolk_Island" title="Kingston, Norfolk Island">Kingston</a>.
</p>
<div class="mw-kartographer-container thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width: 360px;"><a class="mw-kartographer-map" style="width: 360px; height: 360px;" data-mw="interface" data-style="osm-intl" data-width="360" data-height="360" data-zoom="11" data-overlays="[&quot;_0bb229278cf32e2efd8b3fcf86f3457bf0744282&quot;]" href="/wiki/Special:Map/11/a/a/en"><img src="https://maps.wikimedia.org/img/osm-intl,11,a,a,360x360.png?lang=en&amp;domain=%2F%2Fen.wikipedia.org&amp;title=Norfolk+Island&amp;revid=1133740137&amp;groups=_0bb229278cf32e2efd8b3fcf86f3457bf0744282" alt="" width="360" height="360" decoding="async" srcset="https://maps.wikimedia.org/img/osm-intl,11,a,a,360x360@2x.png?lang=en&amp;domain=%2F%2Fen.wikipedia.org&amp;title=Norfolk+Island&amp;revid=1133740137&amp;groups=_0bb229278cf32e2efd8b3fcf86f3457bf0744282 2x" /></a><div class="thumbcaption">Norfolk Island is the main island in a group comprising the Australian <a href="/wiki/States_and_territories_of_Australia" title="States and territories of Australia">external territory</a> of Norfolk Island, situated in the <a href="/wiki/Pacific_Ocean" title="Pacific Ocean">Pacific Ocean</a> between <a href="/wiki/Australia" title="Australia">Australia</a> and <a href="/wiki/New_Zealand" title="New Zealand">New Zealand</a> approximately 692&#160;km (430&#160;mi) south of <a href="/wiki/New_Caledonia" title="New Caledonia">New Caledonia</a>.</div></div></div>
<p>The first known settlers in Norfolk Island were East <a href="/wiki/Polynesia" title="Polynesia">Polynesians</a> but they had already departed when <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Great_Britain" title="Kingdom of Great Britain">Great Britain</a> settled it as part of its 1788 settlement of Australia. The island served as a <a href="/wiki/Penal_colony" title="Penal colony">convict penal settlement</a> from 6 March 1788 until 5 May 1855, except for an 11-year hiatus between 15 February 1814 and 6 June 1825,<sup id="cite_ref-:4_11-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:4-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:5_12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:5-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> when it lay abandoned. On 8 June 1856, permanent civilian residence on the island began when descendants of the <a href="/wiki/Mutiny_on_the_Bounty" title="Mutiny on the Bounty"><i>Bounty</i> mutineers</a> were relocated from <a href="/wiki/Pitcairn_Islands" title="Pitcairn Islands">Pitcairn Island</a>. In 1914 the UK handed Norfolk Island over to Australia to administer as an <a href="/wiki/Department_of_External_Territories_(1941%E2%80%9351)" class="mw-redirect" title="Department of External Territories (1941–51)">external territory</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Roberts-Wray_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Roberts-Wray-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Native to the island, the evergreen <a href="/wiki/Araucaria_heterophylla" title="Araucaria heterophylla">Norfolk Island pine</a> is a symbol of the island and is pictured on its <a href="/wiki/Flag_of_Norfolk_Island" title="Flag of Norfolk Island">flag</a>. The pine is a key export for Norfolk Island, being a popular ornamental tree in Australia (where two related species grow), and also worldwide.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: History">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/History_of_Norfolk_Island" title="History of Norfolk Island">History of Norfolk Island</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_settlement">Early settlement</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Early settlement">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Norfolk Island was uninhabited when first settled by Europeans, but evidence of earlier habitation was obvious. Archaeological investigation suggests that in the 13th or 14th century the island was settled by East <a href="/wiki/Polynesia" title="Polynesia">Polynesian</a> seafarers, either from the <a href="/wiki/Kermadec_Islands" title="Kermadec Islands">Kermadec Islands</a> north of New Zealand, or from the <a href="/wiki/North_Island" title="North Island">North Island</a> of New Zealand. However, both Polynesian and <a href="/wiki/Melanesians" title="Melanesians">Melanesian</a> artefacts have been found, so it is possible that people from <a href="/wiki/New_Caledonia" title="New Caledonia">New Caledonia</a>, relatively close to the north, also reached Norfolk Island. Human occupation must have ceased at least a few hundred years before Europeans arrived in the late 18th century. Ultimately, the relative isolation of the island, and its poor <a href="/wiki/Horticulture" title="Horticulture">horticultural</a> environment, were not favourable to long-term settlement.<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="First_penal_settlement_.281788.E2.80.931814.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="First_penal_settlement_(1788–1814)">First penal settlement (1788–1814)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: First penal settlement (1788–1814)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Norfolk_Island_Foundation_Day" title="Norfolk Island Foundation Day">Norfolk Island Foundation Day</a></div>
<p>The first European known to have sighted and landed on the island was Captain <a href="/wiki/James_Cook" title="James Cook">James Cook</a>, on 10 October 1774,<sup id="cite_ref-:4_11-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:4-11">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-:5_12-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:5-12">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> on his second voyage to the South Pacific on <a href="/wiki/HMS_Resolution_(1771)" title="HMS Resolution (1771)">HMS&#160;<i>Resolution</i></a>. He named it after <a href="/wiki/Mary_Howard,_Duchess_of_Norfolk_(died_1773)" title="Mary Howard, Duchess of Norfolk (died 1773)">Mary Howard, Duchess of Norfolk</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> Sir <a href="/wiki/John_Call" title="John Call">John Call</a> argued the advantages of Norfolk Island in that it was uninhabited and that <a href="/wiki/Phormium" title="Phormium">New Zealand flax</a> grew there.
</p><p>After the outbreak of the <a href="/wiki/American_Revolutionary_War" title="American Revolutionary War">American Revolutionary War</a> in 1776 halted <a href="/wiki/Penal_transportation" title="Penal transportation">penal transportation</a> to the <a href="/wiki/Thirteen_Colonies" title="Thirteen Colonies">Thirteen Colonies</a>, British prisons started to <a href="/wiki/Prison_overcrowding" title="Prison overcrowding">overcrowd</a>. Several stopgap measures proved ineffective, and the government announced in December 1785 that it would send convicts to parts of what is now known as Australia. In 1786, it included Norfolk Island as an auxiliary settlement, as proposed by John Call, in its plan for colonisation of the <a href="/wiki/Colony_of_New_South_Wales" title="Colony of New South Wales">Colony of New South Wales</a>. The decision to settle Norfolk Island was taken after Empress <a href="/wiki/Catherine_the_Great" title="Catherine the Great">Catherine II of Russia</a> restricted the sale of <a href="/wiki/Hemp" title="Hemp">hemp</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> At the time, practically all the hemp and flax required by the Royal Navy for cordage and sailcloth was imported from Russia.
</p><p>When the <a href="/wiki/First_Fleet" title="First Fleet">First Fleet</a> arrived at <a href="/wiki/Port_Jackson" title="Port Jackson">Port Jackson</a> in January&#160;1788, Governor <a href="/wiki/Arthur_Phillip" title="Arthur Phillip">Arthur Phillip</a> ordered Lieutenant <a href="/wiki/Philip_Gidley_King" title="Philip Gidley King">Philip Gidley King</a> to lead a party of 15&#160;convicts and seven free men to take control of Norfolk Island, and prepare for its commercial development. They arrived on 6 March. During the first year of the settlement, which was also called "Sydney" like its parent, more convicts and soldiers were sent to the island from New South Wales. Robert Watson, harbourmaster, arrived with the First Fleet as quartermaster of <a href="/wiki/HMS_Sirius_(1786)" title="HMS Sirius (1786)">HMS&#160;<i>Sirius</i></a>, and was still serving in that capacity when the ship was wrecked at Norfolk Island in 1790. Next year, he obtained and cultivated a grant of 60 acres (24&#160;ha) on the island.<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As early as 1794, Lieutenant-Governor of New South Wales <a href="/wiki/Francis_Grose_(British_Army_officer)" title="Francis Grose (British Army officer)">Francis Grose</a> suggested its closure as a penal settlement, as it was too remote and difficult for shipping and too costly to maintain.<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> The first group of people left in February&#160;1805, and by 1808, only about 200&#160;remained, forming a small settlement until the remnants were removed in 1813. A small party remained to slaughter stock and destroy all buildings, so that there would be no inducement for anyone, especially from other European powers, to visit and lay claim to the place. From February&#160;1814 until June&#160;1825, the island was uninhabited.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Norfolk_Island_jail1.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/20/Norfolk_Island_jail1.jpg/220px-Norfolk_Island_jail1.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="145" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/20/Norfolk_Island_jail1.jpg/330px-Norfolk_Island_jail1.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/20/Norfolk_Island_jail1.jpg/440px-Norfolk_Island_jail1.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1000" data-file-height="657" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Norfolk_Island_jail1.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The Old Military Barracks in <a href="/wiki/Kingston,_Norfolk_Island" title="Kingston, Norfolk Island">Kingston</a></div></div></div>
<h3><span id="Second_penal_settlement_.281824.E2.80.931856.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Second_penal_settlement_(1824–1856)">Second penal settlement (1824–1856)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Second penal settlement (1824–1856)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Norfolk_Island_jail.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/67/Norfolk_Island_jail.jpg/220px-Norfolk_Island_jail.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="144" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/67/Norfolk_Island_jail.jpg/330px-Norfolk_Island_jail.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/67/Norfolk_Island_jail.jpg/440px-Norfolk_Island_jail.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1200" data-file-height="783" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Norfolk_Island_jail.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Remains of Norfolk Island <a href="/wiki/Gaol" class="mw-redirect" title="Gaol">gaol</a></div></div></div>
<p>In 1824 the British government instructed the Governor of New South Wales, <a href="/wiki/Thomas_Brisbane" title="Thomas Brisbane">Thomas Brisbane</a>, to reoccupy Norfolk Island as a place to send "the worst description of convicts". Its remoteness, previously seen as a disadvantage, was now viewed as an asset for the detention of recalcitrant male prisoners. The convicts detained have long been assumed to be hardcore recidivists, or 'doubly-convicted capital respites' – that is, men transported to Australia who committed fresh crimes in the colony for which they were sentenced to death, but were spared the gallows on condition of life on Norfolk Island. However, a 2011 study, using a database of <span class="nowrap"><span data-sort-value="7003645800000000000♠"></span>6458</span> Norfolk Island convicts, has demonstrated that the reality was somewhat different: more than half were detained on Norfolk Island without ever receiving a colonial conviction, and only 15% had been reprieved from a death sentence. Furthermore, the overwhelming majority of convicts sent to Norfolk Island had committed non-violent property offences, and the average length of detention there was three years.<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> Nonetheless, Norfolk Island went through periods of unrest with convicts staging a number of <a href="/wiki/Norfolk_Island_convict_mutinies" title="Norfolk Island convict mutinies">uprisings and mutinies</a> between 1826 and 1846, all of which failed.<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> The British government began to wind down the second penal settlement after 1847, and the last convicts were removed to <a href="/wiki/Tasmania" title="Tasmania">Tasmania</a> in May 1855. The island was abandoned because transportation from the United Kingdom to <a href="/wiki/Van_Diemen%27s_Land" title="Van Diemen&#39;s Land">Van Diemen's Land</a> (Tasmania) had ceased in 1853, to be replaced by <a href="/wiki/Penal_labour" title="Penal labour">penal servitude</a> in the UK.
</p>
<h3><span id="Settlement_by_Pitcairn_Islanders_.281856.E2.80.93present.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Settlement_by_Pitcairn_Islanders_(1856–present)">Settlement by Pitcairn Islanders (1856–present)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Settlement by Pitcairn Islanders (1856–present)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:252px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Descendants_of_the_mutineers,_1862.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Descendants_of_the_mutineers%2C_1862.jpg/250px-Descendants_of_the_mutineers%2C_1862.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="186" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Descendants_of_the_mutineers%2C_1862.jpg/375px-Descendants_of_the_mutineers%2C_1862.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ea/Descendants_of_the_mutineers%2C_1862.jpg/500px-Descendants_of_the_mutineers%2C_1862.jpg 2x" data-file-width="578" data-file-height="429" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Descendants_of_the_mutineers,_1862.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Descendants of the mutineers John Adams and Matthew Quintal on Norfolk Island, 1862. From Left to right:John Adams 1827–1897 son of George Adams; John Quintal 1820–1912 son of Arthur Quintal; George Adams 1804–1873 son of John Adams; Arthur Quintal 1795–1873 son of Matthew Quintal</div></div></div>
<p>The next settlement began on 8 June 1856, as the descendants of Tahitians and the <a href="/wiki/HMS_Bounty" title="HMS Bounty">HMS <i>Bounty</i></a> mutineers, including those of <a href="/wiki/Fletcher_Christian" title="Fletcher Christian">Fletcher Christian</a>, were resettled from the <a href="/wiki/Pitcairn_Islands" title="Pitcairn Islands">Pitcairn Islands</a>, which had become too small for their growing number. On 3 May 1856, 193 people left Pitcairn Islands aboard the <i>Morayshire</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> On 8 June 194 people arrived, a baby having been born in transit.<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> The Pitcairners occupied many of the buildings remaining from the penal settlements, and gradually established traditional farming and whaling industries on the island. Although some families decided to return to Pitcairn in 1858 and 1863, the island's population continued to grow. They accepted additional settlers, who often arrived on whaling vessels.
</p><p>The island was a regular resort for whaling vessels in the <a href="/wiki/Age_of_Sail" title="Age of Sail">age of sail</a>. The first such ship was the <i>Britannia</i> in November 1793. The last on record was the <i>Andrew Hicks</i> in August–September 1907.<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> They came for water, wood and provisions and sometimes they recruited islanders to serve as crewmen on their vessels.
</p><p>In 1867, the headquarters of the <a href="/wiki/Melanesian_Mission" title="Melanesian Mission">Melanesian Mission</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Church_of_England" title="Church of England">Church of England</a> was established on the island. In 1920 the Mission was relocated from Norfolk Island to the <a href="/wiki/Solomon_Islands" title="Solomon Islands">Solomon Islands</a> to be closer to the focus of population.
</p><p>Norfolk Island was the subject of several experiments in administration during the century. It began the 19th century as part of the Colony of New South Wales. On 29 September 1844, Norfolk Island was transferred from the Colony of New South Wales to the Colony of Van Diemen's Land.<sup id="cite_ref-Norfolk_Island_Act_1913_24-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Norfolk_Island_Act_1913-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: Recital 2">&#58;&#8202;Recital 2&#8202;</span></sup> On 1 November 1856 Norfolk Island was separated from the Colony of Tasmania (formerly Van Diemen's Land) and constituted as a "distinct and separate Settlement, the affairs of which should until further Order in that behalf by Her Majesty be administered by a Governor to be for that purpose appointed".<sup id="cite_ref-Australian_Waste_Lands_Act_25-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Australian_Waste_Lands_Act-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-1856_Order_in_Council_26-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-1856_Order_in_Council-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Governor_of_New_South_Wales" title="Governor of New South Wales">Governor of New South Wales</a> was constituted as the Governor of Norfolk Island.<sup id="cite_ref-Norfolk_Island_Act_1913_24-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Norfolk_Island_Act_1913-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: Recital 3">&#58;&#8202;Recital 3&#8202;</span></sup>
</p><p>On 19 March 1897 the office of the Governor of Norfolk Island was abolished and responsibility for the administration of Norfolk Island was vested in the Governor of the Colony of New South Wales. Yet, the island was not made a part of New South Wales and remained separate. The Colony of New South Wales ceased to exist upon the establishment of the Commonwealth of Australia on 1 January 1901, and from that date responsibility for the administration of Norfolk Island was vested in the Governor of the State of New South Wales.<sup id="cite_ref-Norfolk_Island_Act_1913_24-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Norfolk_Island_Act_1913-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: Recitals 7 and 8">&#58;&#8202;Recitals 7 and 8&#8202;</span></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="20th_century">20th century</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: 20th century">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:302px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Norfolk_Island_Act_1913_Proclamation.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/36/Norfolk_Island_Act_1913_Proclamation.jpg/300px-Norfolk_Island_Act_1913_Proclamation.jpg" decoding="async" width="300" height="319" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/36/Norfolk_Island_Act_1913_Proclamation.jpg/450px-Norfolk_Island_Act_1913_Proclamation.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/36/Norfolk_Island_Act_1913_Proclamation.jpg/600px-Norfolk_Island_Act_1913_Proclamation.jpg 2x" data-file-width="982" data-file-height="1044" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Norfolk_Island_Act_1913_Proclamation.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Norfolk Island Act 1913 Proclamation, effective 1 July 1914</div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Parliament_of_the_Commonwealth_of_Australia" class="mw-redirect" title="Parliament of the Commonwealth of Australia">Parliament of the Commonwealth of Australia</a> accepted the territory by the <i>Norfolk Island Act 1913</i> (Cth),<sup id="cite_ref-Roberts-Wray_13-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Roberts-Wray-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: p 886">&#58;&#8202;p 886&#8202;</span></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Norfolk_Island_Act_1913_24-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Norfolk_Island_Act_1913-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> subject to British agreement; the Act received royal assent on 19 December 1913. In preparation for the handover, a proclamation by the <a href="/wiki/Governor_of_New_South_Wales" title="Governor of New South Wales">Governor of New South Wales</a> on 23 December 1913 (in force when gazetted on 24 December) repealed "all laws heretofore in force in Norfolk Island" and replaced them by re-enacting a list of such laws.<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> Among those laws was the <i>Administration Law 1913</i> (NSW), which provided for appointment of an Administrator of Norfolk Island and of magistrates, and contained a code of criminal law.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>British agreement was expressed on 30 March 1914, in a UK <a href="/wiki/Order_in_Council" title="Order in Council">Order in Council</a><sup id="cite_ref-Proclamation:_Norfolk_Island_Act_1913_29-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Proclamation:_Norfolk_Island_Act_1913-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> made pursuant to the <i>Australian Waste Lands Act</i> 1855 (Imp).<sup id="cite_ref-Australian_Waste_Lands_Act_25-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Australian_Waste_Lands_Act-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Roberts-Wray_13-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Roberts-Wray-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: p 886">&#58;&#8202;p 886&#8202;</span></sup> A proclamation by the <a href="/wiki/Governor-General_of_Australia" title="Governor-General of Australia">Governor-General of Australia</a> on 17 June 1914 gave effect to the Act and the Order as from 1 July 1914.<sup id="cite_ref-Proclamation:_Norfolk_Island_Act_1913_29-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Proclamation:_Norfolk_Island_Act_1913-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>During <a href="/wiki/World_War_II" title="World War II">World War II</a>, the island became a key <a href="/wiki/Norfolk_Island_Airport" title="Norfolk Island Airport">airbase</a> and refuelling depot between Australia and New Zealand, and New Zealand and the <a href="/wiki/Solomon_Islands" title="Solomon Islands">Solomon Islands</a>. The airstrip was constructed by Australian, New Zealand and the United States servicemen during 1942.<sup id="cite_ref-:2_30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> Since Norfolk Island fell within New Zealand's area of responsibility, it was garrisoned by a <a href="/wiki/New_Zealand_Army" title="New Zealand Army">New Zealand Army</a> unit known as <a href="/wiki/N_Force" title="N Force">N Force</a> at a large army camp which had the capacity to house a <span class="nowrap"><span data-sort-value="7003150000000000000♠"></span>1500</span> strong force. N Force relieved a company of the <a href="/wiki/Second_Australian_Imperial_Force" title="Second Australian Imperial Force">Second Australian Imperial Force</a>. The island proved too remote to come under attack during the war and N Force left the island in February 1944.
</p><p>In 1979, Norfolk Island was granted limited self-government by Australia, under which the island elected a government that ran most of the island's affairs.<sup id="cite_ref-Norfolk_Island_Act_1979_31-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Norfolk_Island_Act_1979-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="21st_century">21st century</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: 21st century">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>In 2006, a formal review process took place, in which the Australian government considered revising this model of government. The review was completed on 20 December 2006, when it was decided that there would be no changes in the governance of Norfolk Island.<sup id="cite_ref-ag_32-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ag-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Financial problems and a reduction in tourism led to Norfolk Island's administration appealing to the Australian federal government for assistance in 2010. In return, the islanders were to pay income tax for the first time but would be eligible for greater welfare benefits.<sup id="cite_ref-ABC_News_33-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ABC_News-33">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> However, by May 2013 agreement had not been reached and islanders were having to leave to find work and welfare.<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup> An agreement was finally signed in Canberra on 12 March 2015 to replace self-government with a local council but against the wishes of the Norfolk Island government.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> A majority of Norfolk Islanders objected to the Australian plan to make changes to Norfolk Island without first consulting them and allowing their say, with 68% of voters against forced changes.<sup id="cite_ref-Radio_New_Zealand_37-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Radio_New_Zealand-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>  An example of growing friction between Norfolk Island and increased Australian rule was featured in a 2019 episode of Discovery Channel's annual <a href="/wiki/Shark_Week" title="Shark Week">Shark Week</a>.  The episode featured Norfolk Island's policy of culling growing cattle populations by killing older cattle and feeding the carsasses to tiger sharks well off the coast.  This is done to help prevent tiger sharks from coming further toward shore in search of food.  Norfolk Island holds one of the largest populations of tiger sharks in the world. Australia has banned the culling policy as cruelty to animals.  Norfolk Islanders fear this will lead to increased shark attacks and damage an already waning tourist industry.
</p><p>On 4 October 2015, the time zone for Norfolk Island was changed from <a href="/wiki/UTC%2B11:30" title="UTC+11:30">UTC+11:30</a> to <a href="/wiki/UTC%2B11:00" title="UTC+11:00">UTC+11:00</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Reduced_autonomy_2016">Reduced autonomy 2016</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Reduced autonomy 2016">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>In March 2015, the Australian Government announced comprehensive reforms for Norfolk Island.<sup id="cite_ref-Norfolk_Island_reform_39-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Norfolk_Island_reform-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> The action was justified on the grounds it was necessary "to address issues of sustainability which have arisen from the model of self-government requiring Norfolk Island to deliver local, state and federal functions since 1979".<sup id="cite_ref-Norfolk_Island_reform_39-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Norfolk_Island_reform-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> On 17 June 2015, the Norfolk Island Legislative Assembly was abolished, with the territory becoming run by an Administrator and an advisory council. Elections for a new Regional Council were held on 28 May 2016, with the new council taking office on 1 July 2016.<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>From that date, most Australian Commonwealth laws were extended to Norfolk Island. This means that taxation, social security, immigration, customs and health arrangements apply on the same basis as in mainland Australia.<sup id="cite_ref-Norfolk_Island_reform_39-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Norfolk_Island_reform-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> Travel between Norfolk Island and mainland Australia became domestic travel on 1 July 2016.<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> For the <a href="/wiki/2016_Australian_federal_election" title="2016 Australian federal election">2016 Australian federal election</a>, 328 people on Norfolk Island voted in the <a href="/wiki/Australian_Capital_Territory" title="Australian Capital Territory">ACT</a> <a href="/wiki/Division_of_Canberra" title="Division of Canberra">electorate of Canberra</a>, out of 117,248 total votes.<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> Since 2018, Norfolk Island is covered by the <a href="/wiki/Division_of_Bean" title="Division of Bean">electorate of Bean</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There is opposition to the reforms, led by Norfolk Island People for Democracy Inc., an association appealing to the United Nations to include the island on its list of "<a href="/wiki/United_Nations_list_of_non-self-governing_territories" title="United Nations list of non-self-governing territories">non-self-governing territories</a>".<sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> There has also been movement to join New Zealand since the autonomy reforms.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In October 2019, the Norfolk Island People For Democracy advocacy group conducted a survey of 457 island residents (about one quarter of the entire population) and found that 37% preferred <a href="/wiki/Associated_state" title="Associated state">free association</a> with New Zealand, 35% preferred free association with Australia, 25% preferred <a href="/wiki/Self-governance" title="Self-governance">full independence</a>, and 3% preferred full integration with Australia.<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Geography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1097763485">.mw-parser-output .ambox{border:1px solid #a2a9b1;border-left:10px solid #36c;background-color:#fbfbfb;box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output Unsourced material may be challenged and removed.<br /><small><span class="plainlinks"><i>Find sources:</i>&#160;<a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="//www.google.com/search?as_eq=wikipedia&amp;q=%22Norfolk+Island%22">"Norfolk Island"</a>&#160;–&#160;<a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="//www.google.com/search?tbm=nws&amp;q=%22Norfolk+Island%22+-wikipedia&amp;tbs=ar:1">news</a>&#160;<b>·</b> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="//www.google.com/search?&amp;q=%22Norfolk+Island%22&amp;tbs=bkt:s&amp;tbm=bks">newspapers</a>&#160;<b>·</b> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="//www.google.com/search?tbs=bks:1&amp;q=%22Norfolk+Island%22+-wikipedia">books</a>&#160;<b>·</b> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="//scholar.google.com/scholar?q=%22Norfolk+Island%22">scholar</a>&#160;<b>·</b> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.jstor.org/action/doBasicSearch?Query=%22Norfolk+Island%22&amp;acc=on&amp;wc=on">JSTOR</a></span></small></span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">June 2018</span>)</i></span><span class="hide-when-compact"><i> (<small><a href="/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal" title="Help:Maintenance template removal">Learn how and when to remove this template message</a></small>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:302px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Norfolk_Island_Philip_Island.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cc/Norfolk_Island_Philip_Island.jpg/300px-Norfolk_Island_Philip_Island.jpg" decoding="async" width="300" height="177" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cc/Norfolk_Island_Philip_Island.jpg/450px-Norfolk_Island_Philip_Island.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cc/Norfolk_Island_Philip_Island.jpg/600px-Norfolk_Island_Philip_Island.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1000" data-file-height="591" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Norfolk_Island_Philip_Island.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>View across to <a href="/wiki/Nepean_Island" class="mw-redirect" title="Nepean Island">Nepean Island</a> (foreground) and <a href="/wiki/Phillip_Island_(Norfolk_Island)" title="Phillip Island (Norfolk Island)">Phillip Island</a></div></div></div>
<p>The Territory of Norfolk Island is located in the South Pacific Ocean, east of the Australian mainland. Norfolk Island itself is the main island of the island group that the territory encompasses and is located at <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1073938472"/><span class="plainlinks nourlexpansion"><a class="external text" href="//geohack.toolforge.org/geohack.php?pagename=Norfolk_Island&amp;params=29_02_S_167_57_E_"><span class="geo-default"><span class="geo-dms" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location"><span class="latitude">29°02′S</span> <span class="longitude">167°57′E</span></span></span><span class="geo-multi-punct">&#xfeff; / &#xfeff;</span><span class="geo-nondefault"><span class="geo-dec" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location">29.033°S 167.950°E</span><span style="display:none">&#xfeff; / <span class="geo">-29.033; 167.950</span></span></span></a></span>. It has an area of 34.6 square kilometres (13.4&#160;sq&#160;mi), with no large-scale internal bodies of water and 32&#160;km (20&#160;mi) of coastline. Norfolk was formed from several volcanic eruptions between 3.1 and 2.3 million years ago.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Norfolk_Island-CIA_WFB_Map.png" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1f/Norfolk_Island-CIA_WFB_Map.png/220px-Norfolk_Island-CIA_WFB_Map.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="237" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/1/1f/Norfolk_Island-CIA_WFB_Map.png 1.5x" data-file-width="325" data-file-height="350" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Norfolk_Island-CIA_WFB_Map.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Map of Norfolk Island</div></div></div>
<p>The island's highest point is <a href="/wiki/Mount_Bates" title="Mount Bates">Mount Bates</a> reaching 319 metres (1,047 feet) <a href="/wiki/Above_mean_sea_level" class="mw-redirect" title="Above mean sea level">above sea level</a>, located in the northwest quadrant of the island. The majority of the terrain is suitable for farming and other agricultural uses. Phillip Island, the second largest island of the territory, is located at <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1073938472"/><span class="plainlinks nourlexpansion"><a class="external text" href="//geohack.toolforge.org/geohack.php?pagename=Norfolk_Island&amp;params=29_07_S_167_57_E_"><span class="geo-default"><span class="geo-dms" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location"><span class="latitude">29°07′S</span> <span class="longitude">167°57′E</span></span></span><span class="geo-multi-punct">&#xfeff; / &#xfeff;</span><span class="geo-nondefault"><span class="geo-dec" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location">29.117°S 167.950°E</span><span style="display:none">&#xfeff; / <span class="geo">-29.117; 167.950</span></span></span></a></span>, seven kilometres (4.3 miles) south of the main island.
</p><p>The coastline of Norfolk Island consists, to varying degrees, of cliff faces. A downward slope exists towards Slaughter Bay and Emily Bay, the site of the original colonial settlement of Kingston. There are no safe harbour facilities on Norfolk Island, with loading <a href="/wiki/Jetty" title="Jetty">jetties</a> existing at Kingston and Cascade Bay. All goods not domestically produced are brought in by ship, usually to Cascade Bay. Emily Bay, protected from the Pacific Ocean by a small coral reef, is the only safe area for recreational swimming, although surfing waves can be found at Anson and Ball Bays.
</p><p>The climate is subtropical and mild, with little seasonal differentiation. The island is the eroded remnant of a <a href="/wiki/Basalt" title="Basalt">basaltic</a> <a href="/wiki/Volcano" title="Volcano">volcano</a> active around 2.3 to 3&#160;million years ago,<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> with inland areas now consisting mainly of rolling plains. It forms the highest point on the <a href="/wiki/Norfolk_Ridge" title="Norfolk Ridge">Norfolk Ridge</a>, part of the submerged continent <a href="/wiki/Zealandia_(continent)" class="mw-redirect" title="Zealandia (continent)">Zealandia</a>.
</p><p>The area surrounding Mount Bates is preserved as the <a href="/wiki/Norfolk_Island_National_Park" title="Norfolk Island National Park">Norfolk Island National Park</a>. The park, covering around 10% of the land of the island, contains remnants of the forests which originally covered the island, including stands of subtropical <a href="/wiki/Rainforest" title="Rainforest">rainforest</a>.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Government_House,_Norfolk_Island.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/69/Government_House%2C_Norfolk_Island.jpg/220px-Government_House%2C_Norfolk_Island.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="124" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/69/Government_House%2C_Norfolk_Island.jpg/330px-Government_House%2C_Norfolk_Island.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/69/Government_House%2C_Norfolk_Island.jpg/440px-Government_House%2C_Norfolk_Island.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1920" data-file-height="1080" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Government_House,_Norfolk_Island.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Government House, 2015</div></div></div>
<p>The park also includes the two smaller islands to the south of Norfolk Island, <a href="/wiki/Nepean_Island_(Norfolk_Island)" title="Nepean Island (Norfolk Island)">Nepean Island</a> and <a href="/wiki/Phillip_Island_(Norfolk_Island)" title="Phillip Island (Norfolk Island)">Phillip Island</a>. The vegetation of Phillip Island was devastated due to the introduction during the penal era of pest animals such as pigs and rabbits, giving it a red-brown colour as viewed from Norfolk; however, <a href="/wiki/Pest_control" title="Pest control">pest control</a> and remediation work by park staff has recently brought some improvement to the Phillip Island environment.
</p><p>The major settlement on Norfolk Island is <a href="/wiki/Burnt_Pine" title="Burnt Pine">Burnt Pine</a>, located predominantly along Taylors Road, where the shopping centre, post office, bottle shop, telephone exchange and community hall are located. Settlement also exists over much of the island, consisting largely of widely separated homesteads.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Government_House,_Norfolk_Island" title="Government House, Norfolk Island">Government House</a>, the official residence of the Administrator, is located on Quality Row in what was the penal settlement of Kingston. Other government buildings, including the court, Legislative Assembly and Administration, are also located there. Kingston's role is largely a ceremonial one, however, with most of the economic impetus coming from Burnt Pine.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Climate">Climate</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Climate">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Norfolk Island has a mid-latitude and <a href="/wiki/Humid_subtropical_climate" title="Humid subtropical climate">marine subtropical climate</a> (<a href="/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification" title="Köppen climate classification">Köppen</a>: <i>Cfa</i>). The temperature  never falls below 6&#160;°C (43&#160;°F) or rises above 30&#160;°C (86&#160;°F). The absolute maximum recorded temperature is 28.4&#160;°C (83.1&#160;°F), while the absolute minimum is 6.2&#160;°C (43.2&#160;°F).<sup id="cite_ref-bom_51-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bom-51">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> Average annual precipitation is around 1,100 millimetres (43&#160;in),<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> with most rain falling from April to August. Other months receive significant amounts of precipitation as well.
</p>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Environment">Environment</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Environment">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Norfolk Island is part of the <a href="/wiki/Interim_Biogeographic_Regionalisation_for_Australia" title="Interim Biogeographic Regionalisation for Australia">Interim Biogeographic Regionalisation for Australia</a> region "Pacific Subtropical Islands" (PSI), and forms subregion PSI02, with an area of 3,908 hectares (9,660 acres).<sup id="cite_ref-IBRA7_54-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IBRA7-54">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> The country is home to the <a href="/wiki/Norfolk_Island_subtropical_forests" class="mw-redirect" title="Norfolk Island subtropical forests">Norfolk Island subtropical forests</a> terrestrial ecoregion.<sup id="cite_ref-DinersteinOlson2017_55-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DinersteinOlson2017-55">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Flora">Flora</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Flora">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:KermadecNikau.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d4/KermadecNikau.jpg/220px-KermadecNikau.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="330" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d4/KermadecNikau.jpg/330px-KermadecNikau.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d4/KermadecNikau.jpg/440px-KermadecNikau.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2592" data-file-height="3888" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:KermadecNikau.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><i><a href="/wiki/Rhopalostylis_baueri" title="Rhopalostylis baueri">Rhopalostylis baueri</a></i>, a native palm</div></div></div>
<p>Norfolk Island has 174 native plants; 51 of them are <a href="/wiki/Endemism" title="Endemism">endemic</a>. At least 18 of the endemic species are rare or threatened.<sup id="cite_ref-Eoearth_56-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Eoearth-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> The Norfolk Island palm (<i><a href="/wiki/Rhopalostylis_baueri" title="Rhopalostylis baueri">Rhopalostylis baueri</a></i>) and the smooth tree-fern (<i><a href="/wiki/Cyathea_brownii" class="mw-redirect" title="Cyathea brownii">Cyathea brownii</a></i>), the tallest tree-fern in the world,<sup id="cite_ref-Eoearth_56-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Eoearth-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> are common in the Norfolk Island National Park but rare elsewhere on the island. Before European colonisation, most of Norfolk Island was covered with subtropical rain forest, the canopy of which was made of <i><a href="/wiki/Araucaria_heterophylla" title="Araucaria heterophylla">Araucaria heterophylla</a></i> (Norfolk Island pine) in exposed areas, and the <a href="/wiki/Palmaceae" class="mw-redirect" title="Palmaceae">palm</a> <i>Rhopalostylis baueri</i> and <a href="/wiki/Tree_fern" title="Tree fern">tree ferns</a> <i>Cyathea brownii</i> and <i><a href="/wiki/Cyathea_australis" class="mw-redirect" title="Cyathea australis">C. australis</a></i> in moister protected areas. The <a href="/wiki/Understory" title="Understory">understory</a> was thick with <a href="/wiki/Liana" title="Liana">lianas</a> and ferns covering the forest floor. Only one small tract, 5&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (1.9&#160;sq&#160;mi), of rainforest remains, which was declared as the <a href="/wiki/Norfolk_Island_National_Park" title="Norfolk Island National Park">Norfolk Island National Park</a> in 1986.<sup id="cite_ref-Eoearth_56-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Eoearth-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>This forest has been infested with several <a href="/wiki/Introduced_species" title="Introduced species">introduced plants</a>. The cliffs and steep slopes of Mount Pitt supported a community of shrubs, <a href="/wiki/Herbaceous_plant" title="Herbaceous plant">herbaceous plants</a>, and climbers. A few tracts of cliff top and seashore vegetation have been preserved. The rest of the island has been cleared for pasture and housing. Grazing and introduced weeds currently threaten the native flora, displacing it in some areas. In fact, there are more weed species than native species on Norfolk Island.<sup id="cite_ref-Eoearth_56-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Eoearth-56">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Fauna">Fauna</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Fauna">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>As a relatively small and isolated oceanic island, Norfolk has few land birds but a high degree of endemicity among them. Norfolk Island is home to a radiation of about 40 endemic snail species.<sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> Many of the endemic bird species and subspecies have become <a href="/wiki/Extinction" title="Extinction">extinct</a> as a result of massive clearance of the island's native vegetation of <a href="/wiki/Subtropical" class="mw-redirect" title="Subtropical">subtropical</a> <a href="/wiki/Rainforest" title="Rainforest">rainforest</a> for agriculture, hunting and persecution as agricultural pests. The birds have also suffered from the introduction of mammals such as <a href="/wiki/Rat" title="Rat">rats</a>, cats, pigs and goats, as well as from introduced competitors such as <a href="/wiki/Common_blackbird" title="Common blackbird">common blackbirds</a> and <a href="/wiki/Crimson_rosella" title="Crimson rosella">crimson rosellas</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-birdlife15_59-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-birdlife15-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> Although the island is politically part of Australia, many of Norfolk Island's native birds show affinities to those of neighbouring New Zealand, such as the <a href="/wiki/Norfolk_kaka" class="mw-redirect" title="Norfolk kaka">Norfolk kaka</a>, <a href="/wiki/Norfolk_pigeon" title="Norfolk pigeon">Norfolk pigeon</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/Norfolk_boobook" title="Norfolk boobook">Norfolk boobook</a>.
</p><p>Extinctions include that of the endemic Norfolk kaka, <a href="/wiki/Norfolk_ground_dove" title="Norfolk ground dove">Norfolk ground dove</a> and Norfolk pigeon, while of the endemic subspecies the <a href="/wiki/Norfolk_starling" class="mw-redirect" title="Norfolk starling">starling</a>, <a href="/wiki/Norfolk_triller" title="Norfolk triller">triller</a>, <a href="/wiki/Norfolk_thrush" title="Norfolk thrush">thrush</a> and <a href="/wiki/Norfolk_boobook" title="Norfolk boobook">boobook owl</a> are extinct, although the latter's genes persist in a hybrid population descended from the last female. Other endemic birds are the <a href="/wiki/White-chested_white-eye" title="White-chested white-eye">white-chested white-eye</a>, which may be extinct, the <a href="/wiki/Norfolk_parakeet" title="Norfolk parakeet">Norfolk parakeet</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Norfolk_gerygone" title="Norfolk gerygone">Norfolk gerygone</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Slender-billed_white-eye" title="Slender-billed white-eye">slender-billed white-eye</a> and endemic subspecies of the <a href="/wiki/Norfolk_Island_Pacific_robin" class="mw-redirect" title="Norfolk Island Pacific robin">Pacific robin</a> and <a href="/wiki/Norfolk_golden_whistler" title="Norfolk golden whistler">golden whistler</a>. Subfossil bones indicate that a species of <i><a href="/wiki/Coenocorypha" class="mw-redirect" title="Coenocorypha">Coenocorypha</a></i> snipe was also found on the island and is now extinct, but the taxonomic relationships of this are unclear and have not been scientifically described yet.<sup id="cite_ref-birdlife15_59-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-birdlife15-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Norfolk Island Group <a href="/wiki/Nepean_Island" class="mw-redirect" title="Nepean Island">Nepean Island</a> is also home to breeding seabirds. The <a href="/wiki/Providence_petrel" title="Providence petrel">providence petrel</a> was hunted to local extinction by the beginning of the 19th century but has shown signs of returning to breed on <a href="/wiki/Phillip_Island_(Norfolk_Island)" title="Phillip Island (Norfolk Island)">Phillip Island</a>. Other seabirds breeding there include the <a href="/wiki/White-necked_petrel" title="White-necked petrel">white-necked petrel</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kermadec_petrel" title="Kermadec petrel">Kermadec petrel</a>, <a href="/wiki/Wedge-tailed_shearwater" title="Wedge-tailed shearwater">wedge-tailed shearwater</a>, <a href="/wiki/Australasian_gannet" title="Australasian gannet">Australasian gannet</a>, <a href="/wiki/Red-tailed_tropicbird" title="Red-tailed tropicbird">red-tailed tropicbird</a> and <a href="/wiki/Grey_ternlet" class="mw-redirect" title="Grey ternlet">grey ternlet</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Sooty_tern" title="Sooty tern">sooty tern</a> (known locally as the whale bird) has traditionally been subject to seasonal egg harvesting by Norfolk Islanders.<sup id="cite_ref-ANBG082_61-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ANBG082-61">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Norfolk Island, with neighbouring Nepean Island, has been identified by <a href="/wiki/BirdLife_International" title="BirdLife International">BirdLife International</a> as an <a href="/wiki/Important_Bird_Area" title="Important Bird Area">Important Bird Area</a> because it supports the entire populations of white-chested and slender-billed white-eyes, Norfolk parakeets and Norfolk gerygones, as well as over 1% of the world populations of wedge-tailed shearwaters and red-tailed tropicbirds. Nearby Phillip Island is treated as a separate IBA.<sup id="cite_ref-birdlife15_59-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-birdlife15-59">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Norfolk Island also has a botanical garden, which is home to a sizeable variety of plant species.<sup id="cite_ref-ANBG082_61-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ANBG082-61">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> However, the island has only one native mammal, <a href="/wiki/Gould%27s_wattled_bat" title="Gould&#39;s wattled bat">Gould's wattled bat</a> (<i>Chalinolobus gouldii</i>). It is very rare, and may already be extinct on the island.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Norfolk_swallowtail" class="mw-redirect" title="Norfolk swallowtail">Norfolk swallowtail</a> (<i>Papilio amynthor</i>) is a species of butterfly that is found on Norfolk Island and the <a href="/wiki/Loyalty_Islands" class="mw-redirect" title="Loyalty Islands">Loyalty Islands</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-butterflies_62-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-butterflies-62">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Cetacean" class="mw-redirect" title="Cetacean">Cetaceans</a> were historically abundant around the island as commercial hunts on the island were operating until 1956. Today, numbers of larger whales have disappeared, but even today many species such <a href="/wiki/Humpback_whale" title="Humpback whale">humpback whale</a>, <a href="/wiki/Minke_whale" title="Minke whale">minke whale</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sei_whale" title="Sei whale">sei whale</a>, and dolphins can be observed close to shore, and scientific surveys have been conducted regularly. <a href="/wiki/Southern_right_whale" title="Southern right whale">Southern right whales</a> were once regular migrants to Norfolk,<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> but were severely depleted by historical hunts, and further by recent illegal Soviet and Japanese whaling,<sup id="cite_ref-64" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-64">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> resulting in none or very few, if remnants still live, right whales in these regions along with <a href="/wiki/Lord_Howe_Island" title="Lord Howe Island">Lord Howe Island</a>.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Whale_shark" title="Whale shark">Whale sharks</a> can be encountered off the island, too.
</p>
