<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="List_of_endemic_and_extirpated_native_birds">List of endemic and extirpated native birds</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: List of endemic and extirpated native birds">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Norfolk_parakeet" title="Norfolk parakeet">Norfolk parakeet</a>, <i>Cyanoramphus cookii</i> (endangered)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Norfolk_kaka" class="mw-redirect" title="Norfolk kaka">Norfolk kaka</a>, <i>Nestor productus</i> (extinct)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Brown_goshawk" title="Brown goshawk">Brown goshawk</a>, <i>Accipiter fasciatus</i> (extirpated)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Norfolk_pigeon" title="Norfolk pigeon">Norfolk pigeon</a>, <i>Hemiphaga novaseelandiae spadicea</i> (extinct, subspecies of NZ pigeon)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Norfolk_ground_dove" title="Norfolk ground dove">Norfolk ground dove</a>, <i>Aloepecoenas norfolkensis</i> (extinct)</li>
<li><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_snipe&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Norfolk snipe (page does not exist)">Norfolk snipe</a>, <i>Coenocorypha spp.</i> (extinct, undescribed)</li>
<li><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_rail&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Norfolk rail (page does not exist)">Norfolk rail</a>, <i>Gallirallus spp.</i> (extinct, undescribed)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Norfolk_robin" title="Norfolk robin">Norfolk robin</a>, <i>Petroica multicolor</i> (endangered)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Norfolk_golden_whistler" title="Norfolk golden whistler">Norfolk golden whistler</a>, <i>Pachycephala pectoralis xanthoprocta</i> (vulnerable, subspecies of golden whistler)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Norfolk_triller" title="Norfolk triller">Norfolk triller</a>, <i>Lalage leucopyga leucopyga</i> (extinct, nominate subspecies of long-tailed triller)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Norfolk_Island_thrush" class="mw-redirect" title="Norfolk Island thrush">Norfolk Island thrush</a>, <i>Turdus poliocephalus poliocephalus</i> (extinct, nominate subspecies of Island thrush)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Norfolk_Island_starling" class="mw-redirect" title="Norfolk Island starling">Norfolk Island starling</a>, <i>Aplonis fusca fusca</i> (extinct, nominate subspecies of extinct Tasman starling)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Norfolk_boobook" title="Norfolk boobook">Norfolk boobook</a>, <i>Ninox novaeseelandiae undulata</i> (extinct except for hybrids with nominate subspecies, subspecies of Morepork\Southern boobook)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/White-chested_white-eye" title="White-chested white-eye">White-chested white-eye</a>, <i>Zosterops albogularis</i> (critically endangered, possibly extinct)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Slender-billed_white-eye" title="Slender-billed white-eye">Slender-billed white-eye</a>, <i>Zosterops tenuirostris</i> (near threatened)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Norfolk_gerygone" title="Norfolk gerygone">Norfolk gerygone</a>, <i>Gerygone modesta</i> (near threatened)</li>
<li><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_grey_fantail&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Norfolk grey fantail (page does not exist)">Norfolk grey fantail</a>, <i>Rhiphidura albiscapa pelzelni</i> (least concern, subspecies of grey fantail)</li>
<li><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_petrel&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Norfolk petrel (page does not exist)">Norfolk petrel</a>, <i>Pterodroma spp.</i> (extinct, undescribed)</li></ul>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Demographics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Norfolk_Islanders" title="Norfolk Islanders">Norfolk Islanders</a></div>
<p>The population of Norfolk Island was 2,188 in the 2021 census,<sup id="cite_ref-ABS2021_6-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ABS2021-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> which had declined from a high of 2,601 in 2001.
</p><p>In 2011, residents were 78% of the census count, with the remaining 22% being visitors. 16% of the population were 14&#160;years and under, 54% were 15 to 64&#160;years, and 24% were 65&#160;years and over. The figures showed an ageing population, with many people aged 20–34 having moved away from the island.<sup id="cite_ref-2011census_65-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2011census-65">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Most islanders are of either <a href="/wiki/European_Australians" title="European Australians">European</a>-only (mostly British) or combined European-<a href="/wiki/Tahitians" title="Tahitians">Tahitian</a> ancestry, being descendants of the <a href="/wiki/Mutiny_on_the_Bounty" title="Mutiny on the Bounty"><i>Bounty</i> mutineers</a> as well as more recent arrivals from Australia and New Zealand. About half of the islanders can trace their roots back to <a href="/wiki/Pitcairn_Islands" title="Pitcairn Islands">Pitcairn Island</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-bbc_66-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>This common heritage has led to a limited number of surnames among the islanders – a limit constraining enough that the island's telephone directory also includes nicknames for many subscribers, such as Carrots, Dar Bizziebee, Diddles, Geek, Lettuce Leaf, Possum, Pumpkin, Smudgie, Truck and Wiggy.<sup id="cite_ref-bbc_66-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><b>Population</b>
</p>
<ul><li><span class="nowrap"><span data-sort-value="7003174800000000000♠"></span>1748</span> (as of the 2016 census)</li></ul>
<p><b>Population growth rate</b>
</p>
<ul><li>0.01%</li></ul>
<p><b>Ancestry</b><sup id="cite_ref-:0_68-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-68">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<ul><li>Australian (22.8%)</li>
<li>English (22.4%)</li>
<li>Pitcairn Islander (20%)</li>
<li>Scottish (6%)</li>
<li>Irish (5.2%)</li></ul>
<p><b>Citizenship</b> (as of the 2011 census)
</p>
<ul><li>Australia 79.5%</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/New_Zealand" title="New Zealand">New Zealand</a> 13.3%</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Fiji" title="Fiji">Fiji</a> 2.5%</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Philippines" title="Philippines">Philippines</a> 1.1%</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/United_Kingdom" title="United Kingdom">United Kingdom</a> 1%</li>
<li>Other 1.8%</li>
<li>Unspecified 0.8%</li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Religion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>62% of the islanders are Christians. After the death of the first chaplain Rev G. H. Nobbs in 1884, a <a href="/wiki/Methodism" title="Methodism">Methodist</a> church was formed and in 1891 a <a href="/wiki/Seventh-day_Adventist_Church" title="Seventh-day Adventist Church">Seventh-day Adventist</a> congregation led by one of Nobbs' sons. Some unhappiness with G. H. Nobbs, the more organised and formal ritual of the <a href="/wiki/Church_of_England" title="Church of England">Church of England</a> service arising from the influence of the Melanesian Mission, decline in spirituality, the influence of visiting American whalers, literature sent by Christians overseas impressed by the Pitcairn story, and the adoption of Seventh-day Adventism by the descendants of the mutineers still on Pitcairn, all contributed to these developments.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Roman Catholic Church</a> began work in 1957 and in the late 1990s a group left the former Methodist (then Uniting Church) and formed a <a href="/wiki/Charismatic_Christianity" title="Charismatic Christianity">charismatic</a> fellowship. In 2011, 34% of the ordinary residents identified as <a href="/wiki/Anglican_Church_of_Australia" title="Anglican Church of Australia">Anglican</a>, 13% as <a href="/wiki/Uniting_Church_in_Australia" title="Uniting Church in Australia">Uniting Church</a>, 12% as Roman Catholic and 3% as Seventh-day Adventist. 9% were from other religions. 24% had no religion, and 7% did not indicate a religion.<sup id="cite_ref-2011census_65-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2011census-65">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> Typical ordinary congregations in any church do not exceed 30 local residents as of 2010<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>. The three older denominations have good facilities. Ministers are usually short-term visitors.
</p><p>Statistics:<sup id="cite_ref-CIAPAPUANEWGUINEA_69-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIAPAPUANEWGUINEA-69">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Protestant" class="mw-redirect" title="Protestant">Protestant</a> 49.6%
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Anglican_Church_of_Australia" title="Anglican Church of Australia">Anglican</a> 31.8%</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Uniting_Church_in_Australia" title="Uniting Church in Australia">Uniting Church in Australia</a> 10.6%</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Seventh-Day_Adventist" class="mw-redirect" title="Seventh-Day Adventist">Seventh-Day Adventist</a> 3.2%</li></ul></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Roman Catholic</a> 11.7%</li>
<li>Other 8.6%</li>
<li>None 23.5%</li>
<li>Unspecified 6.6%</li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Country_of_birth">Country of birth</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Country of birth">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>All information below is from the 2016 Census.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_68-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-68">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<ul><li>Australia (39.7%)</li>
<li>Norfolk Island (22.1%)</li>
<li>New Zealand (17.6%)</li>
<li>Fiji (2.7%)</li>
<li>England (2.6%)</li>
<li>Philippines (2.3%)</li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Language">Language</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Language">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Islanders speak both English and a <a href="/wiki/Creole_language" title="Creole language">creole language</a> known as <a href="/wiki/Norfuk_language" title="Norfuk language">Norfuk</a>, a blend of 18th-century English and <a href="/wiki/Tahitian_language" title="Tahitian language">Tahitian</a>, based on <a href="/wiki/Pitkern_language" title="Pitkern language">Pitkern</a>. The Norfuk language is decreasing in popularity as more tourists visit the island, and more young people leave for work and education. However, efforts are being made to keep it alive via dictionaries and the renaming of some tourist attractions to their Norfuk equivalents.
</p><p>In 2004, an act of the Norfolk Island Assembly made Norfuk a co-official language of the island.<sup id="cite_ref-language_act_3-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-language_act-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup> The act is long-titled: "An Act to recognise the Norfolk Island Language (Norf'k) as an official language of Norfolk Island". The "language known as 'Norf'k'" is described as the language "that is spoken by descendants of the first free settlers of Norfolk Island who were descendants of the settlers of Pitcairn Island". The act recognises and protects use of the language but does not require it; in official use, it must be accompanied by an accurate translation into English.<sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> 32% of the total population reported speaking a language other than English in the 2011 census, and just under three-quarters of the ordinarily resident population could speak Norfuk.<sup id="cite_ref-2011census_65-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2011census-65">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><b>Languages</b><sup id="cite_ref-:0_68-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-68">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>All information below is from the 2016 Census.
</p>
<ul><li>English (45.5%)</li>
<li>Norfuk (40.9%)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Fijian_language" title="Fijian language">Fijian</a> (2.0%)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Tagalog_language" title="Tagalog language">Tagalog</a> (1%)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Filipino_language" title="Filipino language">Filipino</a> (0.8%)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Mandarin_Chinese" title="Mandarin Chinese">Mandarin Chinese</a> (0.7%)</li>
<li><b>Population who speaks a non-English language at home (51.7%)</b></li>
<li><b>Population that only speaks English at home (45.5%)</b></li></ul>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Education">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:NorfolkIslandCentral_School1.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1d/NorfolkIslandCentral_School1.jpg/220px-NorfolkIslandCentral_School1.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1d/NorfolkIslandCentral_School1.jpg/330px-NorfolkIslandCentral_School1.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1d/NorfolkIslandCentral_School1.jpg/440px-NorfolkIslandCentral_School1.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4032" data-file-height="3024" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:NorfolkIslandCentral_School1.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Norfolk Island Central School</div></div></div>
<p>The sole school on the island, Norfolk Island Central School, provides education from <a href="/wiki/Pre-primary" class="mw-redirect" title="Pre-primary">kindergarten</a> through to Year 12. The school has a <a href="/wiki/Contractual" class="mw-redirect" title="Contractual">contractual</a> arrangement referred to as a <a href="/wiki/Memorandum_of_Understanding" class="mw-redirect" title="Memorandum of Understanding">Memorandum of Understanding</a> with the New South Wales <a href="/wiki/Department_of_Education_(New_South_Wales)" title="Department of Education (New South Wales)">Department of Education</a> regarding the teaching staff of the school, the latest of which took effect in January 2015.<sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> In 2015 enrolment at the Norfolk Island Central School was <span class="nowrap"><span data-sort-value="7002282000000000000♠"></span>282</span> students.<sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Children on the island learn English as well as Norfuk, in efforts to revive the language.<sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>No public tertiary education infrastructure exists on the island. The Norfolk Island Central School works in partnership with <a href="/wiki/Registered_Training_Organisation" class="mw-redirect" title="Registered Training Organisation">Registered Training Organisations</a> (RTOs) and local employers to support students accessing <a href="/wiki/Registered_training_organisation#Vocational_Education_and_Training_(VET)_Quality_Framework" title="Registered training organisation">Vocational Education and Training</a> (VET) courses.<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Literacy" title="Literacy">Literacy</a> is not recorded officially, but can be assumed to be roughly at a par with Australia's literacy rate, as islanders attend a school which uses a <a href="/wiki/New_South_Wales" title="New South Wales">New South Wales</a> curriculum, before traditionally moving to the mainland for further study.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1097763485"/><table class="box-More_citations_needed plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-Refimprove" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><a href="/wiki/File:Question_book-new.svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/50px-Question_book-new.svg.png" decoding="async" width="50" height="39" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/75px-Question_book-new.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/100px-Question_book-new.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="512" data-file-height="399" /></a></div></td><td class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This section <b>needs additional citations for <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">verification</a></b>.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Please help <a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit">improve this article</a> by <a href="/wiki/Help:Referencing_for_beginners" title="Help:Referencing for beginners">adding citations to reliable sources</a>. Unsourced material may be challenged and removed.<br /><small><span class="plainlinks"><i>Find sources:</i>&#160;<a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="//www.google.com/search?as_eq=wikipedia&amp;q=%22Norfolk+Island%22">"Norfolk Island"</a>&#160;–&#160;<a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="//www.google.com/search?tbm=nws&amp;q=%22Norfolk+Island%22+-wikipedia&amp;tbs=ar:1">news</a>&#160;<b>·</b> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="//www.google.com/search?&amp;q=%22Norfolk+Island%22&amp;tbs=bkt:s&amp;tbm=bks">newspapers</a>&#160;<b>·</b> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="//www.google.com/search?tbs=bks:1&amp;q=%22Norfolk+Island%22+-wikipedia">books</a>&#160;<b>·</b> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="//scholar.google.com/scholar?q=%22Norfolk+Island%22">scholar</a>&#160;<b>·</b> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.jstor.org/action/doBasicSearch?Query=%22Norfolk+Island%22&amp;acc=on&amp;wc=on">JSTOR</a></span></small></span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">December 2021</span>)</i></span><span class="hide-when-compact"><i> (<small><a href="/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal" title="Help:Maintenance template removal">Learn how and when to remove this template message</a></small>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>While there was no "indigenous" culture on the island at the time of settlement, the Tahitian influence of the Pitcairn settlers has resulted in some aspects of Polynesian culture being adapted to that of Norfolk, including the <a href="/wiki/Hula" title="Hula">hula</a> dance. Local cuisine also shows influences from the same region.
</p><p>Islanders traditionally spend a lot of time outdoors, with fishing and other aquatic pursuits being common pastimes, an aspect which has become more noticeable as the island becomes more accessible to tourism. Most island families have at least one member involved in primary production in some form.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Norfolk_Island_Philip_Island3.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/79/Norfolk_Island_Philip_Island3.jpg/220px-Norfolk_Island_Philip_Island3.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="118" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/79/Norfolk_Island_Philip_Island3.jpg/330px-Norfolk_Island_Philip_Island3.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/79/Norfolk_Island_Philip_Island3.jpg/440px-Norfolk_Island_Philip_Island3.jpg 2x" data-file-width="900" data-file-height="483" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Norfolk_Island_Philip_Island3.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>View across to Phillip Island</div></div></div>
<p>Religious observance remains an important part of life for some islanders, particularly the older generations, but actual attendance is about 8% of the resident population plus some tourists. In the 2006 census 19.9% had no religion<sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> compared with 13.2% in 1996.<sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> Businesses are closed on Wednesday and Saturday afternoons and Sundays.<sup id="cite_ref-:2_30-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-30">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>One of the island's long-term residents was the novelist <a href="/wiki/Colleen_McCullough" title="Colleen McCullough">Colleen McCullough</a>, whose works include <i><a href="/wiki/The_Thorn_Birds" title="The Thorn Birds">The Thorn Birds</a></i> and the <i><a href="/wiki/Masters_of_Rome" title="Masters of Rome">Masters of Rome</a></i> series as well as <i><a href="/wiki/Morgan%27s_Run" title="Morgan&#39;s Run">Morgan's Run</a></i>, set, in large part, on Norfolk Island. Ruth Park, notable author of <i><a href="/wiki/The_Harp_in_the_South" title="The Harp in the South">The Harp in the South</a></i> and many other works of fiction, also lived on the island for several years after the death of her husband, writer D'Arcy Niland. Actress/singer <a href="/wiki/Helen_Reddy" title="Helen Reddy">Helen Reddy</a> also moved to the island in 2002, and maintained a house there.<sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>American novelist <a href="/wiki/James_A._Michener" title="James A. Michener">James A. Michener</a>, who served in the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Navy" title="United States Navy">United States Navy</a> during World War II, set one of the chapters of his episodic novel <i><a href="/wiki/Tales_of_the_South_Pacific" title="Tales of the South Pacific">Tales of the South Pacific</a></i> on Norfolk Island.
</p><p>The island is one of the few locations outside North America to celebrate the holiday of <a href="/wiki/Thanksgiving" title="Thanksgiving">Thanksgiving</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Government_and_politics">Government and politics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Government and politics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_Norfolk_Island" title="Politics of Norfolk Island">Politics of Norfolk Island</a></div>
<p>Norfolk Island is the only non-mainland <a href="/wiki/States_and_territories_of_Australia" title="States and territories of Australia">Australian territory</a> to have had self-governance. The <i><a href="/wiki/Norfolk_Island_Act_1979" title="Norfolk Island Act 1979">Norfolk Island Act 1979</a></i>, passed by the <a href="/wiki/Parliament_of_Australia" title="Parliament of Australia">Parliament of Australia</a> in 1979, is the Act under which the island was governed until the passing of the <i>Norfolk Island Legislation Amendment Act</i> 2015 (Cth).<sup id="cite_ref-Norfolk_Island_Legislation_Amendment_Act_2015_82-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Norfolk_Island_Legislation_Amendment_Act_2015-82">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Australia" class="mw-redirect" title="Government of Australia">Australian government</a> maintains authority on the island through an Administrator, currently <a href="/wiki/Eric_Hutchinson_(politician)" title="Eric Hutchinson (politician)">Eric Hutchinson</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-hutchinson_83-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hutchinson-83">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup> From 1979 to 2015, a <a href="/wiki/Norfolk_Island_Legislative_Assembly" title="Norfolk Island Legislative Assembly">Legislative Assembly</a> was elected by popular vote for terms of not more than three years, although legislation passed by the Australian Parliament could extend its laws to the territory at will, including the power to override any laws made by the assembly.
</p><p>The Assembly consisted of nine seats, with electors casting nine equal votes, of which no more than two could be given to any individual candidate. It is a method of voting called a "weighted <a href="/wiki/First_past_the_post" class="mw-redirect" title="First past the post">first past the post</a> system". Four of the members of the Assembly formed the <a href="/wiki/Executive_Council_(Commonwealth_countries)" class="mw-redirect" title="Executive Council (Commonwealth countries)">Executive Council</a>, which devised policy and acted as an advisory body to the Administrator. The last Chief Minister of Norfolk Island was <a href="/wiki/Lisle_Snell" title="Lisle Snell">Lisle Snell</a>. Other ministers included: Minister for Tourism, Industry and Development; Minister for Finance; Minister for Cultural Heritage and Community Services; and Minister for Environment.
</p><p>All seats were held by independent candidates. Norfolk Island did not embrace party politics. In 2007, a branch of the <a href="/wiki/Australian_Labor_Party" title="Australian Labor Party">Australian Labor Party</a> was formed on Norfolk Island, with the aim of reforming the system of government.
</p><p>Since 2018, residents of Norfolk Island have been required to enroll in the <a href="/wiki/Division_of_Bean" title="Division of Bean">Division of Bean</a>. As is the case for all Australian citizens, enrolment and voting for Norfolk Islanders is compulsory.<sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Disagreements over the island's relationship with Australia were put in sharper relief by a 2006 review undertaken by the Australian government.<sup id="cite_ref-ag_32-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ag-32">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> Under the more radical of two models proposed in the review, the island's legislative assembly would have been reduced to the status of a <a href="/wiki/Local_government_in_Australia" title="Local government in Australia">local council</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-bbc_66-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup> However, in December 2006, citing the "significant disruption" that changes to the governance would impose on the island's economy, the Australian government ended the review leaving the existing governance arrangements unaltered.<sup id="cite_ref-review_85-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-review-85">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In a move that apparently surprised many islanders, the Chief Minister of Norfolk Island, David Buffett, announced on 6 November 2010 that the island would voluntarily surrender its self-government status in return for a financial bailout from the federal government to cover significant debts.<sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>It was announced on 19 March 2015 that self-governance for the island would be revoked by the Commonwealth and replaced by a local council with the state of <a href="/wiki/New_South_Wales" title="New South Wales">New South Wales</a> providing services to the island. A reason given was that the island had never gained self-sufficiency and was being heavily subsidised by the Commonwealth, being given $12.5 million in 2015 alone. It meant that residents would have to start paying Australian income tax, but they would also be covered by Australian welfare schemes such as Centrelink and Medicare.<sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Norfolk Island Legislative Assembly decided to hold a referendum on the proposal. On 8 May 2015, voters were asked if Norfolk Islanders should freely determine their political status and their economic, social and cultural development, and to "be consulted at referendum or plebiscite on the future model of governance for Norfolk Island before such changes are acted upon by the Australian parliament".<sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> 68% out of 912 voters voted in favour. The Norfolk Island Chief Minister, Lisle Snell, said that "the referendum results blow a hole in Canberra's assertion that the reforms introduced before the Australian Parliament that propose abolishing the Legislative Assembly and Norfolk Island Parliament were overwhelmingly supported by the people of Norfolk Island".<sup id="cite_ref-Radio_New_Zealand_37-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Radio_New_Zealand-37">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <i>Norfolk Island Legislation Amendment Act 2015</i> passed the <a href="/wiki/Australian_Parliament" class="mw-redirect" title="Australian Parliament">Australian Parliament</a> on 14 May 2015 (assented on 26 May 2015), abolishing self-government on Norfolk Island and transferring Norfolk Island into a <a href="/wiki/Council" title="Council">council</a> as part of New South Wales law.<sup id="cite_ref-Norfolk_Island_Legislation_Amendment_Act_2015_82-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Norfolk_Island_Legislation_Amendment_Act_2015-82">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup> From 1 July 2016 Norfolk Island legislation will be transferred to New South Wales and subject to NSW legislation.<sup id="cite_ref-Norfolk_Island_reform_39-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Norfolk_Island_reform-39">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Manual_of_Style/Dates_and_numbers#Chronological_items" title="Wikipedia:Manual of Style/Dates and numbers"><span title="Was it transferred? (November 2019)">needs update</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The island's official capital is <a href="/wiki/Kingston,_Norfolk_Island" title="Kingston, Norfolk Island">Kingston</a>; it is, however, more a centre of government than a sizeable settlement. The largest settlement is at <a href="/wiki/Burnt_Pine" title="Burnt Pine">Burnt Pine</a>.
</p><p>The most important local holiday is <a href="/wiki/Bounty_Day" title="Bounty Day">Bounty Day</a>, celebrated on 8 June, in memory of the arrival of the Pitcairn Islanders in 1856.
</p><p>Local ordinances and acts apply on the island, where most laws are based on the Australian legal system. Australian common law applies when not covered by either Australian or Norfolk Island law. <a href="/wiki/Suffrage" title="Suffrage">Suffrage</a> is universal at age eighteen.
</p><p>As a territory of Australia, Norfolk Island does not have diplomatic representation abroad, or within the territory, and is also not a participant in any international organisations, other than sporting organisations.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Flag_of_Norfolk_Island" title="Flag of Norfolk Island">flag</a> is three vertical bands of green, white, and green with a large green Norfolk Island pine tree centred in the slightly wider white band.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Norfolk_Island_Regional_Council" title="Norfolk Island Regional Council">Norfolk Island Regional Council</a> was established in July 2016 to govern the territory at the local level in line with <a href="/wiki/Local_government_in_Australia" title="Local government in Australia">local governments in mainland Australia</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Constitutional_status">Constitutional status</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Constitutional status">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>From 1788 until 1844, Norfolk Island was a part of the <a href="/wiki/Colony_of_New_South_Wales" title="Colony of New South Wales">Colony of New South Wales</a>. In 1844, it was severed from New South Wales and annexed to the Colony of <a href="/wiki/Van_Diemen%27s_Land" title="Van Diemen&#39;s Land">Van Diemen's Land</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Norfolk_Island_Act_1913_24-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Norfolk_Island_Act_1913-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: Recital 2">&#58;&#8202;Recital 2&#8202;</span></sup> With the demise of the third settlement and in contemplation that the inhabitants of Pitcairn Island would move to Norfolk Island,<sup id="cite_ref-Royal_Commission_1976_89-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Royal_Commission_1976-89">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Newbery_90-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Newbery-90">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> the <i>Australian Waste Lands Act</i> 1855 (Imp), gave the Queen in Council the power to "separate Norfolk Island from the Colony of Van Diemen's Land and to make such provision for the government of Norfolk Island as might seem expedient".<sup id="cite_ref-Australian_Waste_Lands_Act_25-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Australian_Waste_Lands_Act-25">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> In 1856, the Queen in Council ordered that Norfolk Island be a distinct and separate settlement, appointing the Governor of New South Wales to also be the Governor of Norfolk Island with "full power and authority to make laws for the order, peace, and good government" of the island.<sup id="cite_ref-1856_Order_in_Council_26-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-1856_Order_in_Council-26">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> Under these arrangements Norfolk Island was effectively self-governing,<sup id="cite_ref-Kerr_2009_91-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kerr_2009-91">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup> Although Norfolk Island was a colony acquired by settlement, it was never within the <i>British Settlements Act</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-Roberts-Wray_13-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Roberts-Wray-13">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup><sup class="reference nowrap"><span title="Page / location: p 885">&#58;&#8202;p 885&#8202;</span></sup><sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The constitutional status of Norfolk Island was revisited in 1894 when the British Government appointed an inquiry into the administration of justice on the island.<sup id="cite_ref-Kerr_2009_91-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kerr_2009-91">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup> By this time, there had been steps in Australia <a href="/wiki/Constitutional_history_of_Australia#Towards_federation" title="Constitutional history of Australia">towards federation</a> including the <a href="/wiki/Constitutional_Convention_(Australia)#1891_convention" title="Constitutional Convention (Australia)">1891 constitutional convention</a>. There was a correspondence between the Governor of Norfolk Island, the British colonial office and the Governor of New Zealand as to how the island should be governed and by whom. Even within NSW, it was felt that "the laws and system of government in the Colony of New South Wales would not prove suitable to the Island Community".<sup id="cite_ref-Kerr_2009_91-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kerr_2009-91">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup> In 1896, the Governor of New Zealand wrote "I am advised that, as far as my Ministers can ascertain, if any change is to take place in the government of Norfolk Island, the Islanders, while protesting against any change, would prefer to come under the control of New Zealand rather than that of New South Wales".<sup id="cite_ref-Kerr_2009_91-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kerr_2009-91">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The British government decided not to annex Norfolk Island to the Colony of NSW and instead that the affairs of Norfolk Island would be administered by the Governor of NSW in that capacity rather than having a separate office as Governor of Norfolk Island. The order-in-council contemplated the future annexation of Norfolk Island to the Colony of NSW or to any federal body of which NSW form part.<sup id="cite_ref-Kerr_2009_91-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kerr_2009-91">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup> Norfolk Island was not a part of NSW and residents of Norfolk Island were not entitled to have their names placed on the NSW electoral roll.<sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup> Norfolk Island was accepted as a territory of Australia, separate from any state, by the <i>Norfolk Island Act</i> 1913 (Cth),<sup id="cite_ref-Norfolk_Island_Act_1913_24-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Norfolk_Island_Act_1913-24">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> passed under the territories power,<sup id="cite_ref-s122_95-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-s122-95">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup> and made effective in 1914.<sup id="cite_ref-Proclamation:_Norfolk_Island_Act_1913_29-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Proclamation:_Norfolk_Island_Act_1913-29">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> Norfolk Island was given a limited form of self-government by the <i>Norfolk Island Act</i> 1979 (Cth).<sup id="cite_ref-Norfolk_Island_Act_1979_31-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Norfolk_Island_Act_1979-31">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There have been four challenges to the constitutional validity of the Australian Government's authority to administer Norfolk Island:
</p>
<ul><li>In 1939, Samuel Hadley argued that the only valid laws in Norfolk Island were those made under the 1856 Order in Council and that all subsequent laws were invalid; his case was rejected by the <a href="/wiki/High_Court_of_Australia" title="High Court of Australia">High Court</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-1939_jersey_bull_96-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-1939_jersey_bull-96">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>In 1965, the <a href="/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_Norfolk_Island" title="Supreme Court of Norfolk Island">Supreme Court of Norfolk Island</a> rejected Henry Newbery's appeal against conviction for failing to apply to be enrolled to vote in Norfolk Island Council elections. He had argued that in 1857 Norfolk Island had a constitution and a legislature such that the Crown could not abolish the legislature nor place Norfolk Island under the authority of Australia. In the Supreme Court, Eggleston J considered the constitutional history of Norfolk Island and concluded that the <i>Australian Waste Lands Act</i> 1855 (Imp) authorised any form of government, representative or non-representative, and that this included placing Norfolk Island under the authority of Australia.<sup id="cite_ref-Newbery_90-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Newbery-90">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>As a result of the Australian Government's decision in 1972 to prevent Norfolk Island from being used as a tax haven, Berwick Ltd claimed to be resident in Norfolk Island but was convicted of failing to lodge a tax return. One of the arguments for Berwick Ltd was that Norfolk Island, as an external territory, was not part of Australia in the constitutional sense. In 1976, the High Court unanimously rejected this argument, approving the <i>Newbery</i> decision and holding that Norfolk Island was a part of Australia.<sup id="cite_ref-97" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-97">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>In 2004 the Australian Government amended the <i>Norfolk Island Act</i> 1979 (Cth) to remove the right for non-Australian citizens to enrol and stand for election to the Legislative Assembly of Norfolk Island.<sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> The validity of the amendments was challenged in the High Court, arguing that as an external territory Norfolk Island was not part of Australia in the constitutional sense and that disenfranchising residents of Norfolk Island who were not Australian citizens was inconsistent with self-government. In 2007 the High Court of Australia rejected these arguments, again approving the <i>Newbery</i> decision and holding that Norfolk Island was part of Australia and that self-government did not require residency rather than citizenship to determine the entitlement to vote.<sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<p>The Government of Australia thus holds that:
</p>
<dl><dd>Norfolk Island has been an integral part of the Commonwealth of Australia since 1914 when it was accepted as an Australian territory under section 122 of the Constitution. The Island has no international status independent of Australia.<sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup></dd></dl>
<p>Much of the self-government under the 1979 legislation was repealed with effect from 2016.<sup id="cite_ref-Norfolk_Island_Legislation_Amendment_Act_2015_82-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Norfolk_Island_Legislation_Amendment_Act_2015-82">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup> The reforms included, to the chagrin of some of the locals of Norfolk Island, a repeal of the preambular sections of the Act which originally were 3–4 pages recognising the particular circumstances in the history of Norfolk Island.<sup id="cite_ref-101" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-101">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Consistent with the Australian position, the United Nations Decolonization Committee<sup id="cite_ref-102" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-102">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup> does not include Norfolk Island on its list of <a href="/wiki/Non-self-governing_territories" class="mw-redirect" title="Non-self-governing territories">non-self-governing territories</a>.
</p><p>This legal position is disputed by some residents on the island. Some islanders claim that Norfolk Island was actually granted independence at the time <a href="/wiki/Queen_Victoria" title="Queen Victoria">Queen Victoria</a> granted permission to Pitcairn Islanders to re-settle on the island.<sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Following reforms to the status of Norfolk Island, there were mass protests by the local population.<sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> In 2015 it was reported that Norfolk Island was taking its argument for self-governance to the United Nations.<sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup> A campaign to preserve the island's autonomy was formed, named Norfolk's Choice.<sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> A formal petition was lodged with the United Nations by <a href="/wiki/Geoffrey_Robertson" title="Geoffrey Robertson">Geoffrey Robertson</a> on behalf of the local population on 25 April 2016.<sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Various suggestions for retaining the island's self-government have been proposed. In 2006, a UK MP, <a href="/wiki/Andrew_Rosindell" title="Andrew Rosindell">Andrew Rosindell</a>, raised the possibility of the island becoming a self-governing <a href="/wiki/British_Overseas_Territory" class="mw-redirect" title="British Overseas Territory">British Overseas Territory</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup> In 2013, the island's last chief minister, <a href="/wiki/Lisle_Snell" title="Lisle Snell">Lisle Snell</a>, suggested independence, to be supported by income from fishing, offshore banking and foreign aid.<sup id="cite_ref-110" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-110">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The laws of Norfolk Island were in a transitional state, under the Norfolk Island Applied Laws Ordinance 2016 (Cth), from 2016 until 2018.<sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup> Laws of New South Wales as applying in Norfolk Island were suspended (with five major exceptions, which the 2016 Ordinance itself amended) until the end of June 2018. From 1 July 2018, all laws of New South Wales apply in Norfolk Island and, as "applied laws", are subject to amendment, repeal or suspension by federal ordinance.<sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup> The Local Government Act 1993 (NSW) has been amended for application to Norfolk Island.<sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Immigration_and_citizenship">Immigration and citizenship</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Immigration and citizenship">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Permanent_Resident_of_Norfolk_Island_visa" title="Permanent Resident of Norfolk Island visa">Permanent Resident of Norfolk Island visa</a></div>
<p>The island was subject to separate immigration controls from the remainder of Australia. Before 1 July 2016, immigration to Norfolk Island, even by other Australian citizens was heavily restricted.<sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup> In 2012, immigration controls were relaxed with the introduction of an Unrestricted Entry Permit<sup id="cite_ref-FS59_116-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FS59-116">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> for all Australian and New Zealand citizens upon arrival and the option to apply for residency; the only criteria were to pass a police check and be able to pay into the local health scheme.<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup> From 1 July 2016, the Australian migration system <a href="/wiki/Australian_migration_zone#Bringing_Norfolk_Island_within_the_zone" title="Australian migration zone">replaced</a> the immigration arrangements previously maintained by the Norfolk Island Government.<sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup> Holders of Australian visas who travelled to Norfolk Island would have departed the <a href="/wiki/Australian_Migration_Zone" class="mw-redirect" title="Australian Migration Zone">Australian Migration Zone</a> before 1 July 2016. Unless they held a multiple-entry visa, the visa would have ceased; in which case they would require another visa to re-enter mainland Australia.<sup id="cite_ref-FS59_116-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FS59-116">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Australian citizens and residents from other parts of the nation now have an automatic right of residence on the island after meeting these criteria (Immigration (Amendment No. 2) Act 2012). Australian citizens can carry either a passport or a form of photo identification to travel to Norfolk Island. The <a href="/wiki/Document_of_Identity_(Australia)" class="mw-redirect" title="Document of Identity (Australia)">Document of Identity</a>, which is no longer issued, is also acceptable within its validity period. Citizens of all other nations must carry a passport to travel to Norfolk Island even if arriving from other parts of Australia.
</p><p>Non-Australian citizens who are permanent residents of Norfolk Island may apply for Australian citizenship after meeting normal residence requirements and are eligible to take up residence in mainland Australia at any time through the use of a Confirmatory (Residence) visa (subclass 808).<sup id="cite_ref-120" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-120">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup> Children born on Norfolk Island are Australian citizens as specified by <a href="/wiki/Australian_nationality_law" title="Australian nationality law">Australian nationality law</a>.
</p><p>Non-Australian citizens who are <a href="/wiki/Australian_permanent_resident" title="Australian permanent resident">Australian permanent residents</a> should be aware that during their stay on Norfolk Island they are "outside of Australia" for the purposes of the Migration Act. This means that not only will they need a still-valid migrant visa or <a href="/wiki/Resident_return_visa_(Australia)" class="mw-redirect" title="Resident return visa (Australia)">Resident return visa</a> to return from Norfolk Island to the mainland, but also the time spent in Norfolk Island will not be counted for satisfying the residency requirement for obtaining a Resident return visa in the future.<sup id="cite_ref-FS59_116-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FS59-116">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> On the other hand, as far as <a href="/wiki/Australian_nationality_law" title="Australian nationality law">Australian nationality law</a> is concerned, Norfolk Island is a part of Australia, and any time spent by an Australian permanent resident on Norfolk Island will count as time spent in Australia for the purpose of applying for Australian citizenship.<sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Health_care">Health care</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Health care">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island_Hospital&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Norfolk Island Hospital (page does not exist)">Norfolk Island Hospital</a> is the only medical centre on the island. From 1 July 2016, medical treatment on Norfolk Island was covered by <a href="/wiki/Medicare_(Australia)" title="Medicare (Australia)">Medicare</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Pharmaceutical_Benefits_Scheme" title="Pharmaceutical Benefits Scheme">Pharmaceutical Benefits Scheme</a> as it is in Australia. Emergency medical treatment is covered by Medicare or a private health insurer.<sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup> Although the hospital can perform minor surgery, serious medical conditions are not permitted to be treated on the island and patients are flown back to mainland Australia. Air charter transport can cost as much as <a href="/wiki/Australian_dollar" title="Australian dollar">A$</a>30,000, which is covered by the Australian Government. For serious emergencies, <a href="/wiki/Medical_evacuation" title="Medical evacuation">medical evacuations</a> were provided by the <a href="/wiki/Royal_Australian_Air_Force" title="Royal Australian Air Force">Royal Australian Air Force</a>; currently this service is provided by Australian Retrieval Services. The island has one ambulance, staffed by one employed St John Officer and a group of <a href="/wiki/St_John_Ambulance_Australia" title="St John Ambulance Australia">St John Ambulance Australia</a> volunteers.
</p><p>The lack of medical facilities available in most remote communities has a major impact on the health care of Norfolk Islanders.<sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup> As is consistent with other extremely remote regions, many older residents find it impossible to remain on the island when their health falters, many leave their homes and live in New Zealand or Australia to get more complex medical care which they require towards the end of their lives.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Defence_and_law_enforcement">Defence and law enforcement</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Defence and law enforcement">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Defence is the responsibility of the <a href="/wiki/Australian_Defence_Force" title="Australian Defence Force">Australian Defence Force</a>. There are no active military installations or defence personnel on Norfolk Island. The Administrator may request the assistance of the Australian Defence Force if required.
</p><p>Civilian law enforcement and community policing are provided by the <a href="/wiki/Australian_Federal_Police" title="Australian Federal Police">Australian Federal Police</a>. The normal deployment to the island is one <a href="/wiki/Sergeant" title="Sergeant">sergeant</a> and two <a href="/wiki/Constable" title="Constable">constables</a>. These are augmented by five local Special Members who have police powers but are not AFP employees.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Courts">Courts</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: Courts">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Norfolk_Island_Court_of_Petty_Sessions" title="Norfolk Island Court of Petty Sessions">Norfolk Island Court of Petty Sessions</a> is the equivalent of a <a href="/wiki/Australian_court_hierarchy" class="mw-redirect" title="Australian court hierarchy">Magistrates Court</a> and deals with minor criminal, civil or regulatory matters. The Chief Magistrate of Norfolk Island is usually the current Chief Magistrate of the <a href="/wiki/Australian_Capital_Territory" title="Australian Capital Territory">Australian Capital Territory</a>. Three local <a href="/wiki/Justice_of_the_Peace" class="mw-redirect" title="Justice of the Peace">Justices of the Peace</a> have the powers of a Magistrate to deal with minor matters.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_Norfolk_Island" title="Supreme Court of Norfolk Island">Supreme Court of Norfolk Island</a> deals with more serious criminal offences, more complex civil matters, administration of deceased estates and federal laws as they apply to the Territory. The Judges of the Supreme Court of Norfolk Island are generally appointed from among Justices of the <a href="/wiki/Federal_Court_of_Australia" title="Federal Court of Australia">Federal Court of Australia</a> and may sit on the Australian mainland or convene a <a href="/wiki/Circuit_court" title="Circuit court">circuit court</a>. Appeals are to the Federal Court of Australia.
</p><p>As stated by the Legal Profession Act 1993,<sup id="cite_ref-124" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-124">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup> "a resident practitioner must hold a Norfolk Island practising certificate." As of 2014<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, only one lawyer maintained a full-time legal practice on Norfolk Island.<sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Census">Census</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: Census">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Until 2016, Norfolk Island took its own censuses, separate from those taken by the <a href="/wiki/Australian_Bureau_of_Statistics" title="Australian Bureau of Statistics">Australian Bureau of Statistics</a> for the remainder of Australia.<sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Postal_service">Postal service</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Postal service">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Prior to 2016, the <a href="/wiki/Norfolk_Island_Postal_Service" title="Norfolk Island Postal Service">Norfolk Island Postal Service</a> was responsible for mail receipt and delivery on the island and issued its own postage stamps. With the merger of Norfolk Island as a regional council, the Norfolk Island Postal Service ceased to exist and all postage is now handled by <a href="/wiki/Australia_Post" title="Australia Post">Australia Post</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup> Australia Post sends and receives mail from Norfolk Island with the postcode 2899.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="State-based_services">State-based services</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: State-based services">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>From 1 January 2022 Queensland will provide state-based services directly for Norfolk Island. Previously New South Wales provided state-based services since July 2016.<sup id="cite_ref-128" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-128">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy_and_infrastructure">Economy and infrastructure</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Economy and infrastructure">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Norfolk_Island_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a3/Norfolk_Island_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/220px-Norfolk_Island_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="143" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a3/Norfolk_Island_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/330px-Norfolk_Island_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a3/Norfolk_Island_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/440px-Norfolk_Island_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1100" data-file-height="715" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Norfolk_Island_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A proportional representation of Norfolk Island exports, 2019</div></div></div>
<p>Tourism, the primary economic activity, has steadily increased over the years. As Norfolk Island prohibits the importation of fresh fruit and vegetables, most produce is grown locally. Beef is both produced locally and imported. The island has one winery, <a href="/wiki/Two_Chimneys_Wines" title="Two Chimneys Wines">Two Chimneys Wines</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-129" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-129">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Australian government controls the <a href="/wiki/Exclusive_economic_zone" title="Exclusive economic zone">exclusive economic zone</a> (EEZ) and revenue from it extending 200 nautical miles (370&#160;km) around Norfolk Island equating to roughly 428,000&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (165,000&#160;sq&#160;mi), and territorial sea claims to 3 nautical miles (5.6&#160;km) from the island. There is a strong belief on the island that some of the revenue generated from Norfolk's EEZ should be available to provide services such as health and infrastructure on the island, which the island has been responsible for, similar to how the Northern Territory is able to access revenue from their mineral resources.<sup id="cite_ref-130" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-130">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup> The exclusive economic zone provides the Islanders with fish, its only major natural resource. Norfolk Island has no direct control over any marine areas but has an agreement with the Commonwealth through the <a href="/wiki/Australian_Fisheries_Management_Authority" title="Australian Fisheries Management Authority">Australian Fisheries Management Authority</a> (AFMA) to fish "recreationally" in a small section of the EEZ known locally as "the Box". While there is speculation that the zone may include oil and gas deposits, this is not proven.<sup id="cite_ref-bbc_66-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup> There are no major arable lands or permanent farmlands, though about 25 percent of the island is a permanent pasture. There is no irrigated land. The island uses the Australian dollar as its currency.
</p><p>In 2015 a company in Norfolk Island was granted a licence to export medicinal cannabis.<sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup> The medicinal cannabis industry has been viewed by some as a means of reinvigorating the economy of Norfolk Island. The Commonwealth stepped in to overturn the decision, with the island's administrator, former Liberal MP Gary Hardgrave revoking the local licence to grow the crop.<sup id="cite_ref-132" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-132">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup> Legislation to allow the cultivation of cannabis in Australia for medical or scientific purposes passed Federal Parliament in February. The Victorian Government will be undertaking a small-scale, strictly controlled cannabis cultivation trial at a Victorian research facility.<sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Taxes">Taxes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Taxes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Formerly, residents of Norfolk Island did not pay Australian federal taxes,<sup id="cite_ref-abc-factfile_134-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-abc-factfile-134">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup> which created a <a href="/wiki/Tax_haven" title="Tax haven">tax haven</a> for locals and visitors alike. There was no <a href="/wiki/Income_tax" title="Income tax">income tax</a> so the island's legislative assembly raised money through an <a href="/wiki/Import_duty" class="mw-redirect" title="Import duty">import duty</a>, fuel levy, medicare levy, <a href="/wiki/Goods_and_services_tax_(Australia)" title="Goods and services tax (Australia)">goods and services tax</a> of 12%, and local/international phone calls.<sup id="cite_ref-bbc_66-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-abc-factfile_134-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-abc-factfile-134">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup> The Chief Minister of Norfolk Island, David Buffett, announced on 6 November 2010 that the island would voluntarily surrender its tax-free status in return for a financial bailout from the federal government to cover significant debts. The introduction of income taxation came into effect on 1 July 2016. Prior to these reforms, residents of Norfolk Island were not entitled to social services.<sup id="cite_ref-135" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-135">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup> It appears that the reforms do extend to companies and trustees and not only individuals.<sup id="cite_ref-136" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-136">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-137" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-137">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Communications">Communications</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: Communications">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>As of 2004<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, <span class="nowrap"><span data-sort-value="7003253200000000000♠"></span>2532</span> telephone main lines are in use, a mix of analog (<span class="nowrap"><span data-sort-value="7003250000000000000♠"></span>2500</span>) and digital (<span class="nowrap"><span data-sort-value="7001320000000000000♠"></span>32</span>) circuits.<sup id="cite_ref-demonym_5-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-demonym-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Communications_satellite" title="Communications satellite">Satellite communications services</a> are planned.<sup id="cite_ref-138" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-138">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup> The island has a locally based radio station (<a href="/wiki/Radio_Norfolk_89.9FM" title="Radio Norfolk 89.9FM">Radio Norfolk</a>), broadcasting on both AM and FM frequencies.<sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup> There is also one television station, Norfolk TV, featuring local programming, plus transmitters for Australian channels <a href="/wiki/ABC_(Australian_TV_channel)" class="mw-redirect" title="ABC (Australian TV channel)">ABC</a>, <a href="/wiki/SBS_(Australian_TV_channel)" title="SBS (Australian TV channel)">SBS</a>, <a href="/wiki/Nine_Network" title="Nine Network">Nine</a> (through <a href="/wiki/Imparja_Television" title="Imparja Television">Imparja Television</a>) and <a href="/wiki/Seven_Network" title="Seven Network">Seven</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Agency2003_140-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Agency2003-140">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup> The Internet <a href="/wiki/Country_code" title="Country code">country code</a> <a href="/wiki/Top-level_domain" title="Top-level domain">top-level domain</a> (<a href="/wiki/CcTLD" class="mw-redirect" title="CcTLD">ccTLD</a>) is <a href="/wiki/.nf" title=".nf">.nf</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-141" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-141">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup> A small <a href="/wiki/GSM" title="GSM">GSM</a> (2G) mobile network operates on the island across 3 towers, however no data transmission is available on this network. An 8-tower 4G/LTE 1800&#160;MHz network was installed in November 2018, improving data service significantly on the island.<sup id="cite_ref-142" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-142">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-143" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-143">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Transport">Transport</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=33" title="Edit section: Transport">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Norfolk_Island_jetty2.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cb/Norfolk_Island_jetty2.jpg/220px-Norfolk_Island_jetty2.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="134" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cb/Norfolk_Island_jetty2.jpg/330px-Norfolk_Island_jetty2.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cb/Norfolk_Island_jetty2.jpg/440px-Norfolk_Island_jetty2.jpg 2x" data-file-width="900" data-file-height="547" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Norfolk_Island_jetty2.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Jetty at Kingston, Norfolk Island</div></div></div>
<p>There are no railways, waterways, ports or harbours on the island.<sup id="cite_ref-144" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-144">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup> Loading <a href="/wiki/Jetty" title="Jetty">jetties</a> are located at Kingston and Cascade, but ships cannot get close to either of them. When a supply ship arrives, it is <a href="/wiki/Lightering" title="Lightering">emptied by whaleboats</a> towed by launches, five tonnes at a time. A mobile crane picks up the freight using nets and straps and lifts the freight onto the pier. Which jetty is used depends on the prevailing weather of the day; the jetty on the leeward side of the island is often used. If the wind changes significantly during unloading/loading, the ship will move around to the other side. Visitors often gather to watch the activity when a supply ship arrives.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (June 2016)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Norfolk Forwarding Services is the primary Freight Forwarding service for Norfolk Island handling both sea and airfreight.  In 2017 Norfolk Forwarding Services shipped most of the freight for the Cascade Pier Project over a period of 18 months.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (September 2018)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The island hosts 80 kilometres (50&#160;mi) of roads, of which 53&#160;km (33&#160;mi) are paved and 27&#160;km (17&#160;mi) unpaved. As with the rest of Australia, driving is on the left side of the road. Uniquely, local law gives livestock right of way.<sup id="cite_ref-bbc_66-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc-66">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup> Speed limits are lower than most mainland Australian roads; the general speed limit is 50&#160;km/h (31&#160;mph), reducing to 40&#160;km/h (25&#160;mph) in town and 30&#160;km/h (19&#160;mph) near schools. Drivers on the island wave to other passing vehicles, this tradition is nicknamed the "Norfolk wave".<sup id="cite_ref-145" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-145">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There is one airport, <a href="/wiki/Norfolk_Island_Airport" title="Norfolk Island Airport">Norfolk Island Airport</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-demonym_5-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-demonym-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> It was formerly serviced by a local airline, <a href="/wiki/Norfolk_Island_Airlines" title="Norfolk Island Airlines">Norfolk Island Airlines</a>, which connected Norfolk Island with Brisbane. As of March 2018, there are no direct flights from New Zealand to Norfolk Island, leaving only services via <a href="/wiki/Sydney" title="Sydney">Sydney</a> and <a href="/wiki/Brisbane" title="Brisbane">Brisbane</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-146" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-146">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> In mid 2018, <a href="/wiki/Air_Chathams" title="Air Chathams">Air Chathams</a> announced it was looking to re-establish flights between Auckland and Norfolk Island<sup id="cite_ref-147" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-147">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup> and in August 2019 announced a weekly service between Auckland and Norfolk Island would begin on 6 September using a <a href="/wiki/Convair_580" class="mw-redirect" title="Convair 580">Convair 580</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-148" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-148">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup>
Following <a href="/wiki/Jacinda_Ardern" title="Jacinda Ardern">Jacinda Ardern's</a> announcement of the opening of the Trans-Tasman bubble, Air Chathams announced a return to service effective from 27 May 2021,<sup id="cite_ref-149" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-149">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup> this service to Auckland, operates on Thursdays using a 36-seater <a href="/wiki/Saab_340" title="Saab 340">Saab 340</a> aircraft.
</p>
<div style="clear:both;"></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Electricity">Electricity</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Norfolk_Island&amp;action=edit&amp;section=34" title="Edit section: Electricity">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Electricity is provided by diesel generators operated by Norfolk Island Electricity, a government organisation. Some electricity is also provided by privately owned rooftop solar panels.<sup id="cite_ref-Electricity_150-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Electricity-150">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
